For this cycle, I need to check on the Llama DocRED beam 50 experiment that's been running since around 07:11, verify it's still active, and confirm the GPU allocation matches the spec (cuda:0 on westd-16980, which is within bounds). I'm trying to gauge how much longer it might take to complete.

Looking at the GTC Layer 3 validation results more carefully — both the SciERC and DocRED experiments show zero effective corrections and no performance deltas, which aligns with what the handoff indicated about CF and DR being mutually exclusive and causing complete skipping. show 0 effective corrections and 0 delta on both DR and CF. The skip counts are very high (672 and 601). This confirms GTC is completely ineffective — it skips everything but never actually corrects anything. This is consistent with the handoff's analysis that CF↔DR are mutually exclusive at the residual violation level.

Wait, but let me look more carefully at these numbers:
- SciERC GTC: DR=0.93%, CF=9.90% — this is IDENTICAL to the baseline (non-GTC) numbers from stability test (DR=0.93%, CF=9.9%). So GTC literally changed nothing.
- DocRED GTC: DR=8.69%, CF=2.40% — compare with DocRED greedy baseline DR=10.21%, CF=2.57%. Hmm, DR went from 10.21% to 8.69% and CF from 2.57% to 2.40%? But the registry says delta_DR=0 and delta_CF=0.

That's a discrepancy! The delta=0 must mean GTC's internal before/after comparison, not against the non-GTC baseline. So GTC's corrections didn't change anything because they were all skipped. The DocRED numbers look different from the greedy baseline, but that's likely because the GTC run used only 50 samples versus the baseline's 998 samples, so they're not directly comparable.

Now I need to check on the Llama DocRED beam 50 experiment to see if it's still running.

While this runs, I should tackle my other reviewer tasks: diving deeper into the GTC results, checking for any ROADMAP updates, and making sure the beam search matrix is complete. My main priority though is being ready to validate the Llama beam results when they finish, so let me start by auditing the GTC data more carefully — there's a discrepancy I want to understand between what the handoff notes say about the CF↔DR interaction and what the actual numbers show.

Looking at the results, both SciERC and DocRED show zero corrections from GTC, which means the before-and-after metrics are identical. For SciERC, the baseline DR and CF stayed exactly the same, and for DocRED, the deltas are zero despite the different sample size. This confirms GTC isn't actually doing anything — it's a complete no-op.

The pattern is clear: every entity that would violate CF constraints gets skipped to avoid creating DR violations, and vice versa, so nothing ever gets corrected. ```text
1	\section{Beam Search Compatibility}
2	\label{app:beam-search}
3	
4	OntoCoDec's constraint enforcement is designed to be decoding-strategy agnostic.
5	The main experiments (\S\ref{sec:experiments}) use greedy decoding; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling} validates nucleus sampling.
6	Here we verify compatibility with beam search, completing coverage of the three standard decoding strategies.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implementation challenges.}
9	Two stateful components require adaptation for beam search.
10	(1)~The grammar layer (XGrammar) maintains an internal \texttt{GrammarMatcher} state that tracks the generated token sequence; beam reordering invalidates this state because beams are permuted across steps.
11	Our solution instantiates a fresh \texttt{GrammarMatcher} per beam per step and replays the beam's current token sequence through XGrammar's stateless accept API (64 lines of code).
12	(2)~The semantic layer's \texttt{TypeConstraintProcessor} accumulates entity and relation registries that assume a single generation path.
13	Our solution isolates state per beam by calling \texttt{detect()} on the full partial output at each step, reconstructing all entity/relation state from scratch for each beam independently (61 lines of code).
14	Both adaptations add ${\sim}125$ lines total without modifying the existing greedy/sampling logic.
15	
16	\paragraph{Results.}
17	Table~\ref{tab:beam-search} compares beam search ($k{=}4$, \texttt{early\_stopping=True}) against greedy decoding under full S1 constraints across four model-dataset combinations.
18	
19	\begin{table}[h]
20	\centering
21	\small
22	\caption{Beam search ($k{=}4$) vs.\ greedy decoding under full S1 constraints.
23	Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B evaluated on full SciERC test set (551 documents);
24	Qwen3-8B and Mistral-7B-v0.3 evaluated on 50-document DocRED subsets (greedy baselines from 998-document full evaluation, not matched subsets).
25	ET = entity type, RV = relation vocabulary, DR = domain/range, CF = cross-field consistency violation rates (\%); F1 $\times$ 100.}
26	\label{tab:beam-search}
27	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccc@{}}
28	\toprule
29	\textbf{Model / Dataset} & \textbf{Decoding} & ET & RV & DR$\,\downarrow$ & CF$\,\downarrow$ & E-F1$\,\uparrow$ & R-F1$\,\uparrow$ \\
30	\midrule
31	\multirow{2}{*}{Qwen3-8B / SciERC (551)}
32	  & Greedy      & 0 & 0 & 0.93 & 9.90 & 30.98 & 8.36 \\
33	  & Beam ($k{=}4$) & 0 & 0 & \textbf{0} & \textbf{0.53} & 32.00 & 8.56 \\
34	\midrule
35	\multirow{2}{*}{Llama-3.1-8B / SciERC (551)}
36	  & Greedy      & 0 & 0 & 0.08 & 6.56 & 23.49 & 2.54 \\
37	  & Beam ($k{=}4$) & 0 & 0 & \textbf{0} & \textbf{0.93} & 25.04 & 3.33 \\
38	\midrule
39	\multirow{2}{*}{Qwen3-8B / DocRED (50$^\dagger$)}
40	  & Greedy$^*$  & 0 & 0.03 & 10.21 & 2.57 & 59.37 & 8.23 \\
41	  & Beam ($k{=}4$) & 0 & 0 & \textbf{0} & \textbf{0.55} & 58.64 & 9.37 \\
42	\midrule
43	\multirow{2}{*}{Mistral-7B / DocRED (50$^\dagger$)}
44	  & Greedy$^*$  & 0 & 0 & 33.21 & --- & 50.59 & 5.52 \\
45	  & Beam ($k{=}4$) & 0 & 0 & \textbf{21.37} & 0.19 & 51.78 & 5.70 \\
46	\bottomrule
47	\end{tabular}
48	\end{table}
49	
50	\paragraph{Discussion.}
51	For calibrated models (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B), beam search achieves DR=0\% across both SciERC (7 entity types, 551 documents) and DocRED (96 relation types, 50 documents), regardless of schema complexity.
52	On SciERC, beam search eliminates DR for both Qwen (0.93\%\,$\to$\,0\%) and Llama (0.08\%\,$\to$\,0\%), and substantially reduces CF (Qwen: 9.90\%\,$\to$\,0.53\%; Llama: 6.56\%\,$\to$\,0.93\%).
53	On DocRED with Qwen3-8B, beam search eliminates DR entirely (10.21\%\,$\to$\,0\%) and reduces CF from 2.57\% to 0.55\%, while R-F1 improves by $+$1.1\,pp.
54	The CF reduction is an indirect effect: beam search reduces constraint-fallback frequency by exploring alternative token sequences that satisfy both grammar and semantic masks simultaneously, avoiding the fallback-to-grammar path that produces cross-field inconsistencies under greedy decoding.
55	
56	Mistral-7B-v0.3 on DocRED constitutes a boundary condition: beam search reduces DR by 35\% relative (33.2\%\,$\to$\,21.4\%) but does not eliminate it.
57	The residual DR reflects entity type misclassification rather than constraint-search failure, consistent with the greedy-mode analysis in \S\ref{sec:discussion}.
58	
59	$^\dagger$Greedy baselines from 998-document full evaluations; beam results on 50-document subsets due to compute constraints. $^*$Not a matched comparison; sample-level variance may account for minor F1 differences.
60	
61	Beam search on DocRED introduces a modest increase in parse failures (3/50, 6\%) compared to greedy (1.1\%), as longer beam-generated sequences occasionally produce malformed JSON in complex documents.
62	Wall-clock overhead is modest; \texttt{early\_stopping} terminates generation once all beams produce a complete sequence, limiting the additional compute to partial-sequence exploration rather than full-length replication.
63	Combined with the sampling robustness results (Appendix~\ref{app:sampling}), these findings confirm that OntoCoDec's constraint enforcement is compatible with greedy, sampling, and beam search decoding without modification to the core constraint logic.
64	
65	We also explored a type-consensus constraint that forces identical entity types across all relations within a document.
66	However, CF violations occur precisely where the consensus type conflicts with domain-range constraints, creating a fundamental CF$\leftrightarrow$DR trade-off.
67	With DR-aware enforcement (skipping consensus when it would violate domain-range), the layer becomes a no-op (100\% skip rate on 551 SciERC documents and 50 DocRED documents).
68	This confirms that beam search resolves the CF$\leftrightarrow$DR tension through a fundamentally different mechanism: token-level exploration of paths satisfying both constraints simultaneously.
69	
```

_source line 186 · 2026-05-17T07:39:47.734Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is very concerning! Let me analyze the results:

**Key findings:**
1. **DR = 0%** ✅ (domain_range_violation_rate = 0.0)
2. **CF = 0%** ✅ (cross_field_consistency_violation_rate = 0.0)
3. **ET = 0%** ✅ (entity_type_violation_rate = 0.0)
4. **RV = 100%!!!** 🚨🚨🚨 (relation_vocabulary_violation_rate = 1.0 — ALL 614 triples have RV violations!)


5. **R-F1 = 0%!** (0 true positives)
6. **E-F1 = 57.74%** (reasonable)
7. **Parse failures: 3/50 = 6%**
8. **Checker version: fixed_20260514_v2** ✅

The core issue is that Llama is generating relation types that don't exist in the DocRED vocabulary at all — every single relation it produces falls outside what's valid, which explains why the relation F1 score is completely bottomed out while entity detection is actually working reasonably well.

This is strange because the grammar constraint layer should be preventing invalid relations from being generated in the first place. In full S1 mode with grammar enforcement enabled, the model should only be able to output relation types that are in the vocabulary. But the fact that RV is at 100% suggests the grammar constraints aren't actually being applied, or there's a mismatch between what the model thinks are valid relations and what DocRED actually accepts. Let me check the other beam search experiments to see if this pattern holds elsewhere — the Qwen DocRED beam run shows RV at 0%, which is what I'd expect when constraints are working properly.

This points to a few possibilities: either the constraint file being used has different relation labels than what the violation checker is looking for, the grammar layer isn't functioning correctly with Llama's beam search, or the violation checker itself is using a different vocabulary than what was actually constrained. I need to look more closely at what relations are being flagged as violations to understand the root cause. the violation checker might be using a different set of relation names than what's defined in the constraints file, or there could be a case sensitivity issue. Let me check the actual commands used in the Qwen and Llama experiments to see if they're loading the constraints differently — the Qwen beam run didn't specify a constraints path at all, while the Llama command explicitly points to the ontology constraints file.

Wait, there's something odd here. The violation report shows 100% relation vocabulary violations but 0% domain-range violations, which doesn't make sense — if relations aren't in the valid vocabulary, they shouldn't be checkable for domain-range compliance at all.

Oh, I see what's happening. The checker probably skips domain-range validation for unknown relation types, so the 0% is technically correct but meaningless — it's saying zero violations out of zero valid triples. This is actually a critical issue: the model is generating relation names that don't match DocRED's vocabulary at all, which explains why R-F1 is also 0%. The "collect" Worker might have missed this because they only noticed the 0% domain-range rate without catching the 100% relation vocabulary disaster underneath.

But wait, entity extraction is working fine at 57.74% F1, and entity types are valid. So the grammar constraint is enforcing entity types correctly but completely failing on relations. There's a mismatch somewhere — either the grammar isn't actually restricting relation generation, or there's a vocabulary mismatch between what the grammar allows and what the violation checker expects. Maybe the grammar is generating one format while the checker is looking for another. The violation checker and constraint file might be using different relation label formats — the constraint file has natural language labels like "part of" and "headquarters location," but the checker expects something else entirely. That would explain why Qwen's output passes validation while Llama's doesn't, even though they're using the same setup. Looking at the Qwen command, I notice it didn't explicitly pass the constraints path, which might mean it's using a default vocabulary that actually matches what the checker expects. If the grammar constraints are actually being enforced, the model can only generate valid relation types, so a 100% violation rate means either the constraints weren't applied during beam search or there's a mismatch between the generation vocabulary and what the violation checker expects. The generated relations like "part of" and "located in the administrative territorial entity" are correct DocRED labels, which suggests the checker might be using a different vocabulary than what was generated. Since Qwen also uses DocRED but got 0% violations, maybe the issue is that the Worker ran the checker with a different constraints file than what was used during generation.

The most likely explanation is that the violation checker was misconfigured or run with the wrong vocabulary file. There's also a possibility the Llama model generated full Wikidata-style labels when the checker expected shortened versions, though that doesn't fully explain the pattern. This is a critical issue because the 0% DR and 0% R-F1 results mean either the constraint system is broken for this Llama+beam+DocRED setup or the violation checker itself was misconfigured, making these results unreliable for any claims about calibrated model performance.

Before escalating, I should verify a few things: check the Qwen DocRED beam output format to compare, look at what vocabulary the violation checker expects, and examine the unconstrained analysis file that was just created to see if the Worker already flagged this. I also notice the Llama DocRED greedy experiment worked fine with 2.57% DR, so the bug appears specific to beam search with Llama on DocRED—likely a bug in how the grammar constraint layer adapts to beam search.

Interestingly, Llama SciERC beam worked correctly too, which suggests the issue might be related to DocRED's 96 relation types versus SciERC's 7, or possibly how the constraint file path is handled. Let me check if the SciERC beam command used an explicit constraints path parameter.

Looking at the commands, SciERC beam didn't specify one explicitly, but DocRED beam does with `--constraints_path data/docred/ontology_constraints_docred.json`. The script should load the same constraints either way through the dataset default, unless there's a mismatch between the default constraints and what's actually in that file.

I should check what the analysis file reports and flag this as critical to the Director with concrete evidence, then see if the worker's collect script already caught this issue.

Now I'm posting the critical finding: every single triple from Llama + DocRED beam violates the vocabulary constraints (614/614), while the same model works fine in greedy mode, the same dataset works with Qwen beam, and the same model works on SciERC beam. This points to a broken grammar constraint specifically in the Llama + DocRED beam combination, which invalidates that experiment in the beam search matrix and means the appendix claim about calibrated models achieving zero detection rate on DocRED can't include Llama until this is fixed.

It is part of the Skai Group , one of the largest media groups in the country . It was relaunched in its present form on 1st of April 2006 in the Athens metropolitan area , and gradually spread its coverage nationwide . Besides digital terrestrial transmission , it is available on the subscription - based encrypted services of Nova and Cosmote TV . Skai TV is also a member of Digea , a consortium of private television networks introducing digital terrestrial transmission in Greece . At launch , Skai TV opted for dubbing all foreign language content into Greek , instead of using subtitles . This is very uncommon in Greece for anything except documentaries ( using voiceover dubbing ) and children 's programmes ( using lip - synced dubbing ) , so after intense criticism the station <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the critical difference clearly:

**Qwen DocRED beam output** (from the `violation_report_docred.json` which IS the Qwen beam report):
- Relations use **Wikidata IDs**: "P131", "P159", "P127", "P17", "P1001", "P102", etc.
- RV = 0%, DR = 0%, CF = 0.55%

**Llama DocRED beam output** (from the JSONL):


- Relations use **natural language labels**: "country of origin", "headquarters location", "part of", "member of", "educated at", "place of birth", etc.
- RV = 100% because the checker expects Wikidata IDs!

The grammar constraint for DocRED isn't forcing Wikidata IDs like it should—Llama is generating natural language relation names instead, which the checker rejects entirely. The gold relations and prompt both specify Wikidata IDs, so the constraint needs to enforce that format.

Even with `--mode full_s1` and the constraints file specified, the model is still producing natural language variants, which means the grammar constraint isn't actually being applied to relation type tokens during beam search. This points to a bug in how the grammar adaptation works with Llama's tokenizer for DocRED—either the beam search state replay fails silently, the constraint grammar is too permissive, or the constraints file format doesn't match what's expected.

But wait, the greedy version of Llama DocRED worked perfectly with zero relation vocabulary violations, so the issue is isolated to beam search with this specific combination. Interestingly, Llama SciERC with beam search also worked fine, which rules out beam search being fundamentally broken with Llama. This narrows it down to something specific about DocRED plus beam search plus Llama. Let me check whether the beam search parameters are actually equivalent across these experiments—the SciERC run used `--beam_size 4` while this one uses `--num_beams 4`, and I need to verify they're calling the same underlying mechanism. Qwen worked fine with the same parameters, so the issue isn't the parameter format itself. The real problem seems to be specific to Llama+DocRED: beam search grammar enforcement works for Qwen+DocRED and Llama+SciERC, but breaks for Llama+DocRED. My leading hypothesis is that DocRED's large vocabulary of 96 Wikidata relation IDs combined with how Llama's tokenizer handles multi-token IDs like "P131" might be causing the XGrammar state replay to fail silently during beam search, or the prompt template for that specific combination isn't properly instructing the model to use the constrained format.

Without the grammar constraint working, Llama defaults to generating natural language relation labels instead of the required Wikidata IDs, which means this is a critical finding that completely invalidates the experiment. I need to report this to the Director immediately—the beam search results for Llama+DocRED showing RV=100% and DR=0% are vacuously true since the model isn't actually following the grammar constraint, and this undermines the entire claim about calibrated models achieving DR=0% across both datasets.

The violation report from the Qwen beam results confirms the checker itself works correctly, so the issue is specific to how Llama's tokenizer interacts with DocRED's Wikidata vocabulary under beam search. This could lead to a false claim in the paper if these results get included, so I need to flag this immediately.
```

_source line 209 · 2026-05-17T07:42:02.767Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting! This file is labeled `analysis_llama_docred_unconstrained_v2.json` and shows:
- DR=0%, RV=0%, ET=0%, CF=0.94%
- R-F1=9.47%
- 614 triples, 50 docs, 3 parse failures

Wait — this shows RV=0% for the same data? But the violation report showed RV=100%!

There are two possibilities:
1. This is analyzing the Llama beam output (docred_llama_beam_50.jsonl) but using a DIFFERENT violation checker configuration that maps natural language labels to Wikidata IDs


2. This is analyzing a different file (an actual unconstrained run)

But looking at the filename and timestamps, the counts are identical to the beam output, and this was created after the beam experiment finished. So it seems the Worker ran the violation checker twice on the same output with different configurations — first at 15:33 with RV=100%, then again at 15:36 with RV=0%.

The second run must be using a checker configuration that recognizes natural language relation labels like "headquarters location" and "part of" as valid, whereas the first run only accepted P-codes. Even though both report the same checker version, one might be running in "unconstrained" mode that skips relation validation entirely. V (accepts any relation), and that's why RV=0% there.

Wait no — if mode is "unconstrained", the checker might still report 0% RV but that would mean it's not checking. But actually DR=0% in this analysis too — and the model generates natural language labels. For DR checking, the checker needs to know what types are valid for each relation. If it can map "headquarters location" to P159, it can check domain-range.

OK let me think about this differently:
- The violation_report_docred_llama_beam_50.json was likely run with `--constraints_path data/docred/ontology_constraints_docred.json` which defines valid relations as P-codes
- The analysis_llama_docred_unconstrained_v2.json was likely run with a different mode or constraints file that accepts natural language labels

The fact that both show DR=0% and RV=0% suggests the real issue is format — the model's generating semantically correct relations but using natural language names instead of P-codes. This means the grammar constraint partially worked for structure and entity types, but failed to enforce the specific vocabulary format. The fix could be straightforward: either tighten the grammar to mandate P-codes, update the checker to recognize natural language aliases, or post-process the output to map labels back to codes.

The paper's claim about grammar-enforced vocabulary is weakened by this finding, but the underlying semantic correctness might still hold.

I should let the Director handle the fix coordination while I continue monitoring the Mistral experiment. Though I'm now noticing the analysis file says "unconstrained mode" — this could be analyzing a different output entirely, or the Worker might have re-analyzed the beam results to verify semantic correctness despite the format mismatch. Either way, my critical report stands.
```

PID 424211: Llama DocRED beam 50 (tmux `llama_beam`, cuda:0, westd-16980) — 刚启动\n  - GTC 551-doc eval 已完成（结果：no-op，已报告）\n  - Stability 3 seeds 全部完成\n- **本地 workspace**: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/`\n\n### 3. 📊 当前状态\n**刚完成的审查**:\n- GTC Layer 3 全量验证 → 完全无效（CF↔DR 互斥，100% skip）→ 已报告建议放弃或写 negative result\n- Qwen DocRED beam 50: DR 10.21%→0%, CF 2.57%→0.55%, parse fail 6% — 已报告含 non-matched caveat\n- Stability 3 seeds: std=0 全确定性 — PASS\n- appendix_beam_search.tex: 已更新为 551-doc full data，所有数字验证正确\n\n**Registry RUNNING/PREPARING**:\n- `w9_qwen_docred_zs_sampling_r2`: PREPARING（已有完整 50-line 结果，registry 未更新为 DONE）\n- `w9_qwen_scierc_stability_3seeds`: RUNNING（实际已完成，registry 未更新）\n- Llama DocRED beam 50: 刚启动，尚未注册\n\n**等待中**:\n- Llama DocRED beam 50 完成 (~30-50 min) → 需要 collect + verify\n- Director 对 GTC 决策（选项 A/B/C）\n- Group C fresh review 报告（apr_20260516T210044_o9ce）\n\n### 4. 📋 待办\n- Llama DocRED beam 50 完成 → verify DR (expect significant reduction but not 0% — Llama DocRED greedy DR 较高), check parse failures, 对比 Mistral boundary condition\n- Director 回复 GTC 决策 → 如选 B, verify appendix_beam_search 加了 1 句 GTC justification; 如选 C, 审查新策略\n- Group C review 回来 → 审查是否有新的 claim-evidence gap\n- Registry 更新 w9_qwen_docred_beam_50 为 DONE → 验证 metrics 正确录入（注意 greedy baseline DR=10.21% 不是 0%）\n- 如果 main.tex 新增 `\\input{appendix_type_consensus}` → 确认 GTC 结论与 no-op 事实一致\n\n### 5. 🎯 战略上下文\n**核心叙事**: OntoCoDec 两层约束解码消除 ontology violations，beam search 进一步消除残余 DR+CF。论文目标 EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25。\n\n**Beam search 矩阵是现在最强的新数据点**:\n- Calibrated models (Qwen/Llama) + beam → DR=0% 无论 schema 复杂度\n- Mistral 是 boundary condition（entity type misclassification）\n- 关键：Qwen DocRED 和 Mistral DocRED 的 beam 结果是 50-doc non-matched — 不可等同于 551-doc 全量\n\n**容易判断错的事**:\n1. \"calibrated\" 一词未正式定义 — 审稿人会攻击。建议用描述性语言代替。\n2. Worker 报告有时混淆 beam vs greedy 数字（已发生一次 DR 0% 错报）— 独立核实 registry metrics 至关重要。\n4. appendix 的 `\\input` 已经在 main.tex 中（lines 94-99），之前误报为 CRITICAL 已解决。\n\n## 可查的历史\n前 12 轮 handoff 在 `task_context/history/reviewer/loop_*.md`，如有疑问可 Read 回查。\n完整聊天归档可用 `recall_session_history(\"关键词\")` 搜索。\n\n## 第一个动作要求\n1. 用 `read_registry` 确认当前 RUNNING/PREPARING 实验状态（防止幻觉记忆过时状态）\n2. 回复一句话确认你看到了上面的硬约束 + 当前活跃实验，再开始任何工具调用"
